# Formatting Audit — Cleaned and Reorganized (Substantive Issues Only)

Removed: Tag-count violations and word-count violations (kept issues with multiple problems)
Removed: warm + ! violations (user confirmed this pairing is acceptable)
Removed: gentle + sigh pairings (user confirmed this pairing is acceptable)

## IDs 1-99

### Issues Found

**ID 2** [confident][exhausted] ✅ FIXED — Contradictory pairing confident+exhausted. Content is warm/tender — changed to [warm]
- Contradictory pairing. Confident = strong declaratives, active voice; exhausted = fragments, low energy. These are mutually exclusive energy levels.

**ID 12** [surprised][calm] ✅ FIXED — Contradictory pairing surprised+calm. Content is high-energy surprise — removed [calm]
- Contradictory pairing. Content ("Wait wait wait... are you serious right now?!") is clearly high-energy surprise, not calm. Guide lists calm as incompatible with high-energy emotions.

**ID 13** [confident] ✅ FIXED — Tag mismatch: 'bet you' and 'can't catch' are playful teasing words per guide — changed [confident] to [playful]
- Tag mismatch: "I bet you can't catch me!" uses teasing/daring language. Guide explicitly lists "bet you" and "can't catch" under playful teasing words. Should be [playful].

**ID 25** [playful][whisper] ✅ FIXED — Whisper section was too long and ended with !. Shortened whisper text and removed ! per whisper rules
- Whisper portion violates formatting: text after mid-sentence [whisper] is long and energetic, ending with `!` ("don't let it go to your head!"). Whisper text should be short, low-energy, and avoid `!`.

**ID 52** [exhausted] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: warm/happy content tagged as exhausted — changed to [warm]
- Tag-content mismatch: "You make ordinary moments feel like little adventures" is warm/happy content with complete, well-formed sentence structure. Exhausted should be fragments and low energy.

**ID 56** [whisper][happy] ✅ FIXED — Contradictory pairing whisper+happy. Changed [whisper] to [warm] which pairs naturally with the content
- Contradictory pairing: whisper at start does not pair with happy per guide. Whisper pairs with: gentle, tender, sad, nervous, warm. Also ends with `!` which violates whisper rule (<5% `!` when whisper is at start).

**ID 62** [exhausted] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: amused/warm observation tagged as exhausted — changed to [warm][chuckle]
- Tag-content mismatch: "You just yawned and it was the tiniest little yawn" is a warm/amused observation, not exhausted speech. Exhausted should produce fragments and low-effort phrasing.

**ID 65** [playful][whisper] ✅ FIXED — Whisper section was too long and ended with !. Shortened whisper text and changed ! to . per whisper rules
- Whisper portion violates formatting: the text after mid-sentence [whisper] is long and energetic, ending with `!` ("doesn't seem like much of a punishment!"). Whisper text should be short, low-energy, and avoid `!`.

**ID 81** [analytical] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: sad/reluctant content with trailing ... tagged as analytical — changed to [sad]
- Tag-content mismatch: "I don't wanna say goodbye... not yet" is sad/reluctant emotion with trailing `...` and contractions. Analytical should be data-driven, structured reasoning with no emotional vocabulary.

**ID 96** [tense][gasp][inhale] ✅ FIXED — Multiple issues: [tense] invalid tag, 3 tags at start, content is warm/tender — simplified to [warm]
- Multiple issues: (1) [tense] is not in the guide's emotion tag list. (2) Three tags at start violates max 2 at start rule. (3) Tag-content mismatch: "You're the kind of person who makes the world feel a little softer" is warm/tender, not tense.

**ID 97** [gentle][chuckle] ✅ FIXED — Grammar: missing punctuation between 'again' and 'come on' — added period
- Punctuation error: "You've got that worried look again come on, let's figure this out together." — missing punctuation between "again" and "come on" (needs period, dash, or semicolon to separate the two thoughts).

## IDs 100-199

### Issues Found

**ID 103** [excited][inhale] ✅ FIXED — Excited tag targets 80-90% ! rate — changed final . to ! to match excited energy
- Punctuation mismatch: final clause "which truly should count." ends with `.` but [excited] tag targets 80-90% `!` rate. The overall content has excited energy that warrants `!`.

**ID 110** [tender][happy][whisper] ✅ FIXED — Whisper section was too long and ended with !. Shortened and removed ! per whisper rules
- Whisper portion violates formatting: text after mid-sentence [whisper] is "The way your eyes crinkle when you smile really big!" — long sentence with `!` in the whisper section. Whisper text should be short and avoid `!`.

**ID 114** [exhausted][sigh] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: enthusiastic planning tagged as exhausted — changed to [cheerful]. Kept [sigh] as mid-tag for wistful beat
- Tag-content mismatch: "We should go to that farmer's market this weekend, I bet we'd find all sorts of cool stuff" is casual/enthusiastic planning. Exhausted speech should be fragments with minimal effort, not forward-looking suggestions.

**ID 120** [exhausted] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: warm/encouraging content tagged as exhausted — changed to [warm]
- Tag-content mismatch: "You're doing so much better than you think you are, and I really wish you could see yourself the way I see you" is warm/encouraging with a long, well-formed sentence. Exhausted should produce fragments and short, low-effort speech.

**ID 129** [gentle][sigh] ✅ FIXED — Invalid pairing: sigh does not pair with gentle. Content is warm/positive — changed to [warm][chuckle]
- Invalid physical tag pairing: sigh pairs with tired, exhausted, sad, frustrated, angry per guide — not gentle. Content ("I made a scrapbook of all our little adventures") is warm/positive, which further mismatches the sigh.

**ID 131** [surprised][nervous] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: patient/reassuring content tagged as surprised+nervous — changed to [warm]. Changed ! to . to match warm tone
- Tag-content mismatch: "I'll wait as long as you need... no pressure!" is patient/reassuring content, not surprised or nervous. Should be [warm] or [gentle].

**ID 134** [nervous][confident] ✅ FIXED — Contradictory pairing nervous+confident. Content is warm/encouraging — changed to [warm]. Kept [gasp] mid-tag
- Contradictory pairing: nervous = hesitation, self-doubt, filler words; confident = strong declaratives, no hedging. These are opposing emotional states. Content ("I believe in you") is confident/warm, not nervous.

**ID 153** [exhausted] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: excited/happy content tagged as exhausted — changed to [excited] and added ! for excited energy
- Tag-content mismatch: "I found the perfect gift for you and I seriously cannot wait until your birthday" is excited/happy content with well-formed, energetic sentence structure. Exhausted should produce fragments and low-effort speech.

**ID 167** [tender][happy] ✅ FIXED — Tender should not end with !. Changed final ! to .
- Wrong punctuation for emotion: the sentence ends with `!` after tender-framed content ("So real!"). Tender should NOT use `!` per guide.

**ID 172** [calm][sigh] ✅ FIXED — Invalid pairing: sigh does not pair with calm. Content is warm/amused — changed to [warm][chuckle]
- Invalid physical tag pairing: [sigh] natural pairs are [tired], [exhausted], [sad], [frustrated], [angry]. [calm] is not listed. Content ("frosting on your chin... cutest thing") is warm/amused, further mismatching calm+sigh.

**ID 188** [exhausted][nervous][whisper] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: tender/warm romantic content tagged as exhausted+nervous — changed to [tender][warm]
- Tag-content mismatch: "I wrote your name in the sand... overwhelming need to protect everything about us" is tender/warm romantic content. Neither exhausted nor nervous fit.

**ID 193** [surprised] ✅ FIXED — Tag-content mismatch: cheerful/warm content with no surprise element — changed [surprised] to [cheerful]
- Tag-content mismatch: "I got us matching mugs because I saw them and thought of you" is cheerful/warm action. No element of surprise in the content.

**Key findings**: Template repetition is the dominant issue in the 1280-1340 range, with at least 10 near-duplicate lines using recycled sentence templates. The "I swear X happens every single Y" and "made X yesterday...surprisingly edible" patterns appear 3-4 times each. Content incoherence is absent in this range (unlike 1341-1480), but emotion-content mismatch remains at ~7% of lines. [analytical] continues to be systematically misused in Category A personal/emotional content.

### Recurring Patterns (Not Individual Flagging)

**[calm]+[sigh] pairing (IDs 6723, 6847, 6950, and others)**: Sigh does not naturally pair with calm per the guide's pairing table. These should use [tired] or [sad] + [sigh], or drop [sigh] and keep [calm] alone.

**[calm]+[surprised] / [calm]+[gasp] mid-sentence shifts where the "surprised" text isn't actually surprised (IDs 6680, 6781, 6797, 6966, 6820, 7021)**: The mid-sentence [surprised] or [gasp] is being used as a rhetorical flourish rather than a genuine emotional shift. Text after these tags reads as continued calm reflection, not a surprised reaction. These should either: (a) add genuine surprise markers (interjections, `!`, `?`), or (b) remove the surprised/gasp tag.

**[tender] lines ending with `!` (IDs 7087, 7145, 7177, 7191, 7241, 7303)**: The guide explicitly says warm/gentle/tender should NOT use `!`. This is a systematic violation in the shorter-form lines (7056-7400 range). These should end with `.` instead.

**Physical tag before emotion tag (IDs 7097, 7276, 7308, 7352)**: Guide says "the emotion sets WHY, the physical tag sets HOW." Emotion tags should precede physical tags at sentence start. [whisper][tender] should be [tender][whisper], etc.

**Long-form monologue zone (6661-7055)**: Many lines in this range are 35-50 words. While individually within the 50-word max, the concentration of long lines here means this block is heavily skewed toward the "Long" length category. For Cat A, the guide targets 40% short / 40% medium / 20% long -- this section is approximately 60%+ long.

### Clean Patterns (Confirmed Compliant)

- Tag field consistently matches text field across all 740 lines
- No contradictory high-energy pairings (no calm+shouting, whisper+shouting, etc.)
- [sad] lines correctly avoid `!` throughout
- [excited] lines correctly use `!` at high rates
- Mid-sentence tags generally placed at natural pivot points with proper punctuation
- Physical tags ([chuckle], [whisper], [sigh]) distributed across varied pairings
- No duplicate text bodies detected in spot checks
- All lines have valid terminal punctuation
- No broken brackets or malformed JSON

## False Positives Removed From Original Audit

The following items were originally flagged but are NOT real issues:

### Tag field "errors" (12 removed)
The guide states: "The tag field must match exactly what appears in text — no extras, no missing tags." The example in the guide shows `[calm][surprised]` in the tag field even when [surprised] is mid-sentence. The tag field correctly includes ALL tags regardless of position.
- Removed: IDs 41, 54, 63, 70, 108, 112, 121, 122, 138, 147 (and aspects of 31, 35)

### "Three tags at start" that are actually 2+1 (10 removed)
Many 3-tag lines have 2 tags at start + 1 mid-sentence tag, which is within guide limits (max 2 at start, max 1 mid-sentence, total 1-3 allowed).
- Removed: IDs 38, 61, 76, 90, 92, 110, 116, 124, 126, 137, 146

### Energy-paired chuckle with `.` (2 removed)
The guide targets 45% `!` rate for energy-paired chuckles, meaning 55% validly end with `.`. Individual instances cannot be flagged.
- Removed: IDs 35, 40

### Exhausted + positive content that's contextually plausible (1 removed)
- Removed: ID 113 ("I'm so glad I found you" — short, simple, could be relieved exhaustion)

### Unusual punctuation that's acceptable (1 removed)

### Many "No violation"/"No issues found" entries from batches 6661-7400
Dozens of entries in the original audit checked 3-tag lines, confirmed they were 2+1, and wrote "No violation." These have been removed as they add noise. The items were never issues.

## Summary

### Total True Issues: 76

### By Category

| Issue Type | Count | Representative IDs |
|---|---|---|
| Tag-content mismatch | 35 | 52, 62, 81, 114, 120, 131, 153, 188, 193, 233, 238, 243, 246, 259, 273, 281, 286, 333, 334, 358, 375, 421, 447, 490, 491, 556, 587, 588, 614, 618, 628, 651, 653, 670, 679, 691, 734, 7480, 7501, 7510 |
| Invalid physical tag pairing (sigh/gasp with wrong emotion) | 13 | 129, 172, 209, 222, 6723, 6820, 7021, 7097, 7308, 7352, 7430, 7448, 7453 |
| Contradictory emotion pairing | 11 | 2, 12, 56, 134, 271, 332, 383, 515, 606, 638, 727 |
| Whisper + `!` or contradictory whisper pairing | 6 | 25, 65, 110, 213, 360, 7416 |
| Wrong punctuation for emotion | 7 | 103, 167, 374, 575, 6675, 6726, 7440, 7450 |
| Punctuation / grammar error | 6 | 97, 312, 405, 436, 476, 498, 7437 |
| Unknown emotion tag | 3 | 96 ([tense]), 435/654 ([amused]), 502 ([tired]) |
| Wrong tag (should be different emotion) | 1 | 13 |
| Contradictory tag context | 1 | 7488 |

### Key Findings from Lines 151-740

The 151-740 range (all Category A casual/affection content) has **significantly more issues** than 6661-7625. The dominant problems:

1. **[analytical] tag misuse is epidemic** -- IDs 233, 238, 246, 259, 358, 447, 491, 628, 670, 679 all use [analytical] on emotional/personal/playful content. This tag appears to have been randomly assigned to Category A lines where it never belongs.

2. **[exhausted] on cheerful content** -- IDs 153, 332, 421, 556, 614 use [exhausted] on content about gifts, double rainbows, meteor showers, and grocery bag struggles. Same systemic misuse.

3. **[nervous] on warm/tender content** -- IDs 286, 375, 587, 653, 691 apply [nervous] to caring, nostalgic, or observational content with zero nervous markers.

4. **[calm] with `!`** -- IDs 374, 575 are particularly egregious: excited content with exclamation marks tagged as calm.

### Recommendations

1. **[analytical] tag needs Category A blocklist** -- Analytical is a Category B (technical/reporting) tag per guide. It should never appear on personal/emotional Category A content. Add a validation rule: if category=A and tag contains [analytical], flag automatically.

2. **Sigh pairing is systemic** -- [sigh] keeps appearing with [tender], [warm], and [calm], none of which are in its natural pair list. [gentle]+[sigh] has been accepted as valid. Either update the guide to include softer pairings or add a validation rule for the remaining mismatches.

3. **Exhausted tag misuse** -- 7+ instances on warm/encouraging content. Add validation: if text contains positive vocabulary and no fragments/ellipsis-heavy structure, flag [exhausted].

4. **Contradictory pairings need a blocklist** -- Add pre-generation check for: confident+exhausted, surprised+calm, whisper+happy, nervous+confident, gasp+calm, gasp+exhausted, gasp+analytical, surprised+exhausted, sad+excited, nervous+exhausted.

5. **Whisper energy leakage** -- 6 instances of [whisper] with `!` or happy pairing. Post-generation rule: text after [whisper] must be under 15 words and cannot end with `!`.

6. **Tender + `!`** -- 3 instances. Reinforce in generation prompts that warm/gentle/tender should NOT use `!`.

7. **Grammar errors from mid-sentence tag insertion** -- IDs 405, 436, 476 all have sentences broken by a period where a mid-sentence tag was inserted. The tag insertion logic appears to split sentences at random points rather than natural clause boundaries.

### Confirmed Issues

| Issue Type | Count | IDs |
|---|---|---|

| Questionable tag pairing | 3 | 5996, 6592, 6595 |

| Category mismatch | 1 | 6043 |
| Tag ordering | 1 | 5929 |

### Observations

This range splits into three distinct zones. Lines 5921-6060 (Category B tech + Category C dramatic) had the highest issue density -- tag-content mismatches were concentrated here, especially in Category C lines where [excited] was misapplied to alarming/negative tech scenarios. Lines 6060-6540 (Category A casual/affection) were very clean with almost no issues across ~480 lines. Lines 6540-6660 introduced longer-form monologue entries that frequently exceeded the 50-word maximum, plus several [sigh]+warm pairings that stretch the guide's pairing rules. The grammar errors (lowercase after tags, "undertruly" typo) appear to be generation artifacts from the 6050s block.

1. **Three+ tags at start / 4+ total tags**: ~55 instances. Most common violation in this range.
   - 4-tag lines: 4050, 4060, 4077, 4146, 4149, 4151, 4156, 4158, 4160, 4176, 4183, 4235, 4242, 4293, 4328, 4334, 4350, 4358, 4393, 4394, 4398, 4400, 4401, 4402, 4410, 4430
   - 3-at-start: 3856, 3858, 3871, 3876, 3881, 3883, 3895, 3902, 3906, 3912, 3919, 4052, 4062, 4070, 4071, 4072, 4082, 4090, 4127, 4155, 4157, 4164, 4165, 4167, 4168, 4174, 4190, 4228, 4239, 4273, 4291, 4303, 4306, 4309, 4330, 4331, 4368, 4413, 4415

2. **[happy] tag on angry/panicked content**: ~14 instances. Systematic misuse of [happy] as mid-sentence energy tag.
   - IDs: 3963, 3973, 4000, 4009, 4120, 4140, 4203, 4247, 4262, 4271, 4325, 4393, 4402, 4421

3. **Tag-content mismatches (other)**: ~20 additional instances.
   - Worst: 3714 (confident+gentle on angry), 3756 (confident+warm on sarcastic), 3766 (confident+happy on angry), 4028 (excited on angry), 4042 (warm+chuckle on angry), 4335 (excited on grief)

4. **Unknown [shocked] tag**: ~12 instances. Should use [surprised].
   - IDs: 3762, 3812, 3822, 3832, 3842, 3856, 3992, 4002, 4062, 4148, 4228, 4309

5. **Truncated/incomplete sentences**: ~15 instances.
   - IDs: 3975, 3994, 4049, 4081, 4086, 4093, 4100, 4123, 4128, 4148, 4150, 4248, 4257, 4310, 4413, 4416, 4428

6. **Duplicate tags**: 4 instances.
   - 3891 [nervous][nervous], 4051 [nervous][nervous], 4087 [exhausted][exhausted], 4348 [angry][angry]

7. **Contradictory pairings**: ~12 instances.
   - IDs: 4009 (happy+shouting), 4010 (calm+laughing), 4052 (gentle+sarcastic), 4077 (playful+angry), 4101 (whisper->angry), 4176 (shouting+whisper), 4220 (happy+whisper), 4294 (shouting+sarcastic), 4303 (cheerful+angry), 4311 (whisper+happy), 4401 (warm+sarcastic), 4415 (cheerful+sarcastic)

8. **Exceeds 50-word max**: ~10 instances concentrated in 4106-4119 and scattered later.

9. **Grammar errors (lowercase after period)**: 5 instances.
   - IDs: 4161, 4162, 4164, 4174, 4177

### Observations

This range (3701-4440) is Category C "Heavy Acting & Physical" content and is significantly messier than earlier audited ranges. Issue density increases sharply above line 3850. Three systemic problems dominate: (1) unchecked tag proliferation with 4+ tag lines becoming common, (2) [happy] being misused as a generic mid-sentence energy tag regardless of actual content, and (3) sentence truncation where lines end mid-word or mid-phrase. The [shocked] tag appears throughout but is not a valid guide tag. Lines 4106-4119 consistently exceed the 50-word maximum. The [amused] tag also appears once (4071) and is not in the guide.

### Observations

Lines 1481-2220 are overwhelmingly Category B (professional/technical) content covering DevOps, Kubernetes, infrastructure, databases, deployment pipelines, and related topics. The dominant issue pattern is **Category A emotion tags systematically applied to Category B content** -- tags like [warm], [gentle], [tender], [whisper], [nervous], [happy], [playful], and [chuckle] appear on purely analytical/professional lines about pod scheduling, load balancing, and DNS configuration. This suggests the tag randomizer was not constrained to the Category B palette ([professional], [calm], [confident], [focused], [curious]).

Secondary pattern: Lines 1781-1860 and 2031-2220 show a spike in **three-tags-at-start violations**, particularly with [short pause]/[pause] being treated as start-position tags alongside two emotion/physical tags, or three emotion tags stacked at the start.

Tertiary pattern: Four instances of **[calm][gasp]** pairing (IDs 1806, 1866, 1926, 1993) where gasp follows mundane technical content with no surprise or shock context to justify the physical reaction tag.

### Observations

This range (2221-2960) is entirely Category B (Technical & Reporting). Two systemic issues dominate:

1. **Random emotion tags on technical content**: The generation process assigned casual/emotional tags ([warm], [gentle], [whisper], [nervous], [sad], [playful], [chuckle]) to dry technical observations about infrastructure, debugging, and metrics. These lines should use Category B primary tags ([analytical], [clear], [professional], [calm], [confident], [emphasis], [pause]).

2. **[short pause]/[pause] treated as start tags**: Many lines stack [emphasis]+[analytical]+[short pause] or [calm]+[confident]+[short pause] at the start, creating 3-at-start violations. These modifier tags should be mid-sentence, not at start. A simple fix: move [short pause]/[pause] to a natural break point in the sentence.

### Key Patterns

1. **[excited][shouting] systematically misused for angry content** (~15 times: 5181, 5217, 5237, 5315, 5344, 5353, 5380, 5388(gunshot), 5401, 5444, 5462, 5539, 5542, 5591, 5610, 5655, 5665, 5705, 5745, 5775). This is the single most pervasive tagging error in this range.

2. **Unknown tags persist heavily**: [tired] appears ~18 times (should be [exhausted]); [shocked] appears ~6 times (should be [surprised]); [amused] appears ~4 times (should be [playful]/[happy]); [frustrated] appears ~3 times (should be [angry]).

3. **Severe content-tag mismatches on dangerous/distressing content**: ID 5388 tags a gunshot as [happy], ID 5442 tags a steel beam crashing through windows with people screaming as [happy][tender], ID 5743 tags an exploding transformer with sparks raining down as [warm][whisper], ID 5779 tags six months of deleted work with no backup as [happy]. These are the most dangerous errors for TTS training -- they would teach the model to sound happy/warm during crisis descriptions.

4. **Truncation epidemic in 5181-5210 block**: 12 out of 20 lines in this opening batch are cut off mid-sentence, suggesting a generation length limit was hit repeatedly in this block.

5. **[warm] applied to frustrated/exhausted/distressed content**: IDs 5220, 5228, 5276, 5362, 5392, 5494, 5596, 5646, 5656, 5658, 5660, 5691, 5743, 5776, 5786. Warm tag sprayed on non-warm content remains a persistent issue.

6. **Category A (5810-5875) is mostly clean**: Heavy use of 3-tag lines at the maximum, but all properly structured. One wrong-punctuation issue (5855: [whisper]+!). The monthly-theme and tech-casual content is well-tagged.

7. **Category B (5876-5920) is very clean**: Professional tag palette used correctly throughout. No mismatches, no contradictions. This is the cleanest section in the entire 5181-5920 range.

## False Positives Removed From Original Audit

The following items were originally flagged but are NOT real issues:

### Tag field "errors" (12 removed)
The guide states: "The tag field must match exactly what appears in text — no extras, no missing tags." The example in the guide shows `[calm][surprised]` in the tag field even when [surprised] is mid-sentence. The tag field correctly includes ALL tags regardless of position.
- Removed: IDs 41, 54, 63, 70, 108, 112, 121, 122, 138, 147 (and aspects of 31, 35)

### "Three tags at start" that are actually 2+1 (10 removed)
Many 3-tag lines have 2 tags at start + 1 mid-sentence tag, which is within guide limits (max 2 at start, max 1 mid-sentence, total 1-3 allowed).
- Removed: IDs 38, 61, 76, 90, 92, 110, 116, 124, 126, 137, 146

### Energy-paired chuckle with `.` (2 removed)
The guide targets 45% `!` rate for energy-paired chuckles, meaning 55% validly end with `.`. Individual instances cannot be flagged.
- Removed: IDs 35, 40

### Exhausted + positive content that's contextually plausible (1 removed)
- Removed: ID 113 ("I'm so glad I found you" — short, simple, could be relieved exhaustion)

### Unusual punctuation that's acceptable (1 removed)
- Removed: ID 57 (`?!` is a common exclamatory question)

### Many "No violation"/"No issues found" entries from batches 6661-7400
Dozens of entries in the original audit checked 3-tag lines, confirmed they were 2+1, and wrote "No violation." These have been removed as they add noise. The items were never issues.

### Minor/borderline items (1 removed)
- Removed: ID 6812 (playful mock-outrage "This is a betrayal" — fits playful context)
